People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Not in the sense of rituals, anyway. I might overthink things sometimes, but I don’t have, like, specific things I feel I have to repeat or do a certain way to make something bad not happen. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any involuntary movements or tics?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I fidget sometimes, but not like movements I can’t control or tics, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” exactly. I mean, I do worry about things sometimes, like anyone, but I don’t know that it feels like anxiety is the main thing. Maybe I’m not explaining that well… does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical sensations like a racing heart, sweating, or trembling when you feel restless?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. When I’m restless it’s more like… I just feel unsettled or like I need to move, but I haven’t noticed my heart racing or sweating or trembling. I mean, not in a way that stands out to me. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I avoid things exactly… but I do put off things where I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork, or talk through household plans. That’s kind of what brought me in, honestly — I feel like I can’t settle in my own body when I’m trying to do those things. I can still do them sometimes, but it feels really uncomfortable, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been noticeably worse since I got married and our routines sort of merged… so, about a year and five months ago. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like it came out of nowhere, but that’s when it started feeling harder to ignore, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or urges to perform specific rituals?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in the sense of rituals, anyway. I might overthink things sometimes, but I don’t have, like, specific things I feel I have to repeat or do a certain way to make something bad not happen. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any involuntary movements or tics?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I fidget sometimes, but not like movements I can’t control or tics, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” exactly. I mean, I do worry about things sometimes, like anyone, but I don’t know that it feels like anxiety is the main thing. Maybe I’m not explaining that well… does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical sensations like a racing heart, sweating, or trembling when you feel restless?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. When I’m restless it’s more like… I just feel unsettled or like I need to move, but I haven’t noticed my heart racing or sweating or trembling. I mean, not in a way that stands out to me. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I avoid things exactly… but I do put off things where I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork, or talk through household plans. That’s kind of what brought me in, honestly — I feel like I can’t settle in my own body when I’m trying to do those things. I can still do them sometimes, but it feels really uncomfortable, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Not like the same scary thought over and over, I don’t think. It’s more that my attention gets pulled away really easily. Like if I’m at the kitchen table trying to get something done, my phone buzzes, or a car goes by outside, or I suddenly think about what we’re having for dinner, and then I’m completely off track before I even realize I stopped. I know that probably sounds normal, but it happens enough that it gets frustrating. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical complaints like pain or discomfort that you haven't mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nothing that feels like a separate physical problem I should be reporting. I get uncomfortable when I’m trying to sit still, but not pain exactly. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you feel compelled to think about over and over, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I can get stuck on things a little if I’m worried I forgot something, but not like I feel forced to think the same thought over and over. It’s more… my mind jumps around, but I don’t have a thought I’m trying to push away that keeps coming back. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or events that seem to trigger your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Mostly when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do the boring-but-important stuff, like paperwork or going over household plans with someone. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s when I really notice this feeling like I can’t quite settle in my own body. Like I should be able to just sit there and handle it, but I get restless really quickly. Is that what you mean by triggers?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thank you. I guess what brings me in is that I’ve been feeling really restless, like I can’t quite settle in my own body. It’s maybe not dramatic, but it’s been getting in the way when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do things like paperwork or talk through household plans. I just feel… uncomfortable and keyed up, I suppose. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been noticeably worse since I got married and our routines sort of merged… so, about a year and five months ago. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like it came out of nowhere, but that’s when it started feeling harder to ignore, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or urges to perform specific rituals?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in the sense of rituals, anyway. I might overthink things sometimes, but I don’t have, like, specific things I feel I have to repeat or do a certain way to make something bad not happen. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any involuntary movements or tics?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I fidget sometimes, but not like movements I can’t control or tics, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” exactly. I mean, I do worry about things sometimes, like anyone, but I don’t know that it feels like anxiety is the main thing. Maybe I’m not explaining that well… does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical sensations like a racing heart, sweating, or trembling when you feel restless?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. When I’m restless it’s more like… I just feel unsettled or like I need to move, but I haven’t noticed my heart racing or sweating or trembling. I mean, not in a way that stands out to me. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I avoid things exactly… but I do put off things where I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork, or talk through household plans. That’s kind of what brought me in, honestly — I feel like I can’t settle in my own body when I’m trying to do those things. I can still do them sometimes, but it feels really uncomfortable, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Not like the same scary thought over and over, I don’t think. It’s more that my attention gets pulled away really easily. Like if I’m at the kitchen table trying to get something done, my phone buzzes, or a car goes by outside, or I suddenly think about what we’re having for dinner, and then I’m completely off track before I even realize I stopped. I know that probably sounds normal, but it happens enough that it gets frustrating. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical complaints like pain or discomfort that you haven't mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nothing that feels like a separate physical problem I should be reporting. I get uncomfortable when I’m trying to sit still, but not pain exactly. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you feel compelled to think about over and over, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Not because I didn’t care—I was trying—but it still affected other people. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or criticize yourself when you can't settle down or complete tasks?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little, yes. manifestation: Waiting in line at the pharmacy or sitting until it’s my turn to speak in a committee meeting makes me tense and fidgety, and I catch myself checking the time over and over.\n- key: What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thank you. I guess what brings me in is that I’ve been feeling really restless, like I can’t quite settle in my own body. It’s maybe not dramatic, but it’s been getting in the way when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do things like paperwork or talk through household plans. I just feel… uncomfortable and keyed up, I suppose. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been noticeably worse since I got married and our routines sort of merged… so, about a year and five months ago. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like it came out of nowhere, but that’s when it started feeling harder to ignore, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or urges to perform specific rituals?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in the sense of rituals, anyway. I might overthink things sometimes, but I don’t have, like, specific things I feel I have to repeat or do a certain way to make something bad not happen. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any involuntary movements or tics?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I fidget sometimes, but not like movements I can’t control or tics, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” exactly. I mean, I do worry about things sometimes, like anyone, but I don’t know that it feels like anxiety is the main thing. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I avoid things exactly… but I do put off things where I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork, or talk through household plans. That’s kind of what brought me in, honestly — I feel like I can’t settle in my own body when I’m trying to do those things. I can still do them sometimes, but it feels really uncomfortable, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Not like the same scary thought over and over, I don’t think. It’s more that my attention gets pulled away really easily. I know that probably sounds normal, but it happens enough that it gets frustrating. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical complaints like pain or discomfort that you haven't mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you're in situations where you have to sit quietly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think fearful, no. Not in the sense of rituals, anyway. I might overthink things sometimes, but I don’t have, like, specific things I feel I have to repeat or do a certain way to make something bad not happen. I can still do them sometimes, but it feels really uncomfortable, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Not like the same scary thought over and over, I don’t think. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Irritable… maybe somewhat, yes. I don’t think I snap at people constantly or anything, but when I’m restless and I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork or household plans, I can feel myself getting tense and short inside.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thank you. I guess what brings me in is that I’ve been feeling really restless, like I can’t quite settle in my own body. It’s maybe not dramatic, but it’s been getting in the way when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do things like paperwork or talk through household plans. I just feel… uncomfortable and keyed up, I suppose. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been noticeably worse since I got married and our routines sort of merged… so, about a year and five months ago. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like it came out of nowhere, but that’s when it started feeling harder to ignore, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or urges to perform specific rituals?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in the sense of rituals, anyway. I might overthink things sometimes, but I don’t have, like, specific things I feel I have to repeat or do a certain way to make something bad not happen. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any involuntary movements or tics?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I fidget sometimes, but not like movements I can’t control or tics, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” exactly. I mean, I do worry about things sometimes, like anyone, but I don’t know that it feels like anxiety is the main thing. Maybe I’m not explaining that well… does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical sensations like a racing heart, sweating, or trembling when you feel restless?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. When I’m restless it’s more like… I just feel unsettled or like I need to move, but I haven’t noticed my heart racing or sweating or trembling. I mean, not in a way that stands out to me. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I avoid things exactly… but I do put off things where I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork, or talk through household plans. That’s kind of what brought me in, honestly — I feel like I can’t settle in my own body when I’m trying to do those things. I can still do them sometimes, but it feels really uncomfortable, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Not like the same scary thought over and over, I don’t think. It’s more that my attention gets pulled away really easily. Like if I’m at the kitchen table trying to get something done, my phone buzzes, or a car goes by outside, or I suddenly think about what we’re having for dinner, and then I’m completely off track before I even realize I stopped. I know that probably sounds normal, but it happens enough that it gets frustrating. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical complaints like pain or discomfort that you haven't mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nothing that feels like a separate physical problem I should be reporting. I get uncomfortable when I’m trying to sit still, but not pain exactly. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you feel compelled to think about over and over, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I can get stuck on things a little if I’m worried I forgot something, but not like I feel forced to think the same thought over and over. It’s more… my mind jumps around, but I don’t have a thought I’m trying to push away that keeps coming back. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or events that seem to trigger your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Mostly when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do the boring-but-important stuff, like paperwork or going over household plans with someone. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s when I really notice this feeling like I can’t quite settle in my own body. Like I should be able to just sit there and handle it, but I get restless really quickly. Is that what you mean by triggers?\n\nDoctor: Has your restlessness affected your relationship with your spouse or other family members since you got married?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t want to blame everything on that, but it has caused some strain. And that’s hard to admit.\n\nIt’s happened in volunteer things too, where I’ve had to step back from responsibilities because I kept letting things fall through. Not because I didn’t care—I was trying—but it still affected other people. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or criticize yourself when you can't settle down or complete tasks?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little, yes. I don’t know if I’d call it harsh criticism exactly, but I do get disappointed in myself when I can’t just sit down and finish something like I’m supposed to. I’ll think, “Why is this so hard?” or feel like I’m letting people down.\n\nI try not to dwell on it too much, but yes, there’s definitely some guilt there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you're in situations where you have to sit quietly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think fearful, no. Anxious… maybe a little sometimes, but I’m not sure that’s the main thing. I get this urge to get up or shift to something else. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but that’s honestly part of why I’m here. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel irritable when you're restless?\n\nPatient: Irritable… maybe somewhat, yes. It’s more like I get uncomfortable or impatient, like I’m trying to make myself stay put and my mind is saying, “Can we be done now?”\n\nI don’t want to overstate it. I’m not usually scared in those situations. It’s more that sitting quietly feels harder than it probably should, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself making excuses to get out of situations where you have to sit quietly?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… though I usually tell myself it’s for a practical reason, not just because I don’t want to sit there.\n\nIt’s more that when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do things like paperwork or go over household plans, I feel like I can’t really settle in my own body. I get this urge to get up or shift to something else. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but that’s honestly part of why I’m here. Not really social gatherings, I don’t think… or at least I wouldn’t say I avoid them just because of restlessness.

Not in the sense of rituals, anyway. I might overthink things sometimes, but I don’t have, like, specific things I feel I have to repeat or do a certain way to make something bad not happen. That’s kind of what brought me in, honestly — I feel like I can’t settle in my own body when I’m trying to do those things. respectful, but I still jump into my husband’s conversations or finish people’s thoughts because the response comes out before I’ve made myself wait.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: Looking back, this was already part of me in elementary school—I was the girl with a messy desk, forgotten permission slips, and notes from teachers saying I talked out of turn or seemed unable to stay seated.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: The fallout has been that people at home and in my volunteer circles can’t reliably count on me, and I’ve had to step back from some responsibilities because I keep letting things fall through despite trying hard.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: It has been noticeably worse since I got married and our routines merged, about a year and five months ago.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 41 / not_in_workforce / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S012\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you find yourself making excuses to get out of situations where you have to sit quietly?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… though I usually tell myself it’s for a practical reason, not just because I don’t want to sit there.\n\nIt’s more that when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do things like paperwork or go over household plans, I feel like I can’t really settle in my own body. I get this urge to get up or shift to something else. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but that’s honestly part of why I’m here. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel irritable when you're restless?\n\nPatient: Irritable… maybe somewhat, yes. I don’t think I snap at people constantly or anything, but when I’m restless and I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork or household plans, I can feel myself getting tense and short inside.\n\nIt’s more like I can’t settle in my own body, and then I get frustrated by that. I don’t know if that counts as irritability exactly, but it does make me feel more on edge.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid social gatherings or meetings because of your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Not really social gatherings, I don’t think… or at least I wouldn’t say I avoid them just because of restlessness.\n\nMeetings or things where I’m expected to sit quietly for a while, though, that’s harder. I may not avoid them outright, but I definitely dread them or try to keep them short. What brings me in is more that feeling like I can’t settle in my own body anymore, especially when I’m supposed to sit and deal with paperwork or shared household plans. I just feel… uncomfortable and keyed up, I suppose. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been noticeably worse since I got married and our routines sort of merged… so, about a year and five months ago. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like it came out of nowhere, but that’s when it started feeling harder to ignore, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or urges to perform specific rituals?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in the sense of rituals, anyway. I might overthink things sometimes, but I don’t have, like, specific things I feel I have to repeat or do a certain way to make something bad not happen. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any involuntary movements or tics?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I fidget sometimes, but not like movements I can’t control or tics, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” exactly. I mean, I do worry about things sometimes, like anyone, but I don’t know that it feels like anxiety is the main thing. Maybe I’m not explaining that well… does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical sensations like a racing heart, sweating, or trembling when you feel restless?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. When I’m restless it’s more like… I just feel unsettled or like I need to move, but I haven’t noticed my heart racing or sweating or trembling. I mean, not in a way that stands out to me. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I avoid things exactly… but I do put off things where I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork, or talk through household plans. That’s kind of what brought me in, honestly — I feel like I can’t settle in my own body when I’m trying to do those things. I can still do them sometimes, but it feels really uncomfortable, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Not like the same scary thought over and over, I don’t think. It’s more that my attention gets pulled away really easily. Like if I’m at the kitchen table trying to get something done, my phone buzzes, or a car goes by outside, or I suddenly think about what we’re having for dinner, and then I’m completely off track before I even realize I stopped. I know that probably sounds normal, but it happens enough that it gets frustrating. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical complaints like pain or discomfort that you haven't mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nothing that feels like a separate physical problem I should be reporting. I get uncomfortable when I’m trying to sit still, but not pain exactly. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you feel compelled to think about over and over, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I can get stuck on things a little if I’m worried I forgot something, but not like I feel forced to think the same thought over and over. It’s more… my mind jumps around, but I don’t have a thought I’m trying to push away that keeps coming back. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or events that seem to trigger your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Mostly when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do the boring-but-important stuff, like paperwork or going over household plans with someone. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s when I really notice this feeling like I can’t quite settle in my own body. Like I should be able to just sit there and handle it, but I get restless really quickly. Is that what you mean by triggers?\n\nDoctor: Has your restlessness affected your relationship with your spouse or other family members since you got married?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t want to blame everything on that, but it has caused some strain. At home, I think people don’t always feel like they can count on me to follow through, even when I really do mean to. And that’s hard to admit.\n\nIt’s happened in volunteer things too, where I’ve had to step back from responsibilities because I kept letting things fall through. Not because I didn’t care—I was trying—but it still affected other people. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or criticize yourself when you can't settle down or complete tasks?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little, yes. I don’t know if I’d call it harsh criticism exactly, but I do get disappointed in myself when I can’t just sit down and finish something like I’m supposed to. I’ll think, “Why is this so hard?” or feel like I’m letting people down.\n\nI try not to dwell on it too much, but yes, there’s definitely some guilt there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you're in situations where you have to sit quietly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think fearful, no. Anxious… maybe a little sometimes, but I’m not sure that’s the main thing. irritable when you're restless?\n\nPatient: Irritable… maybe somewhat, yes. I don’t think I snap at people constantly or anything, but when I’m restless and I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork or household plans, I can feel myself getting tense and short inside.\n\nIt’s more like I can’t settle in my own body, and then I get frustrated by that. I don’t know if that counts as irritability exactly, but it does make me feel more on edge.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid social gatherings or meetings because of your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Not really social gatherings, I don’t think… or at least I wouldn’t say I avoid them just because of restlessness.\n\nMeetings or things where I’m expected to sit quietly for a while, though, that’s harder. I may not avoid them outright, but I definitely dread them or try to keep them short. What brings me in is more that feeling like I can’t settle in my own body anymore, especially when I’m supposed to sit and deal with paperwork or shared household plans. I don’t know if that counts as avoidance exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical symptoms like headaches or stomachaches when you're feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Um… not that I’ve really noticed. I wouldn’t say I get headaches or stomachaches from it, at least not in a clear way.\n\nIt’s more just feeling unsettled or tense, but I don’t think I have those kinds of physical symptoms. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think it’s more… in my body, probably. I mean, emotions get mixed in because I get frustrated with myself, but the main thing is this feeling like I just can’t settle.

Especially if I’m supposed to sit quietly and do paperwork or talk through household plans, I feel like I’m kind of crawling out of my skin. Not dramatically, but enough that it’s hard to stay with it. Does that make sense?

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.1, F42.2, F45.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thank you. I guess what brings me in is that I’ve been feeling really restless, like I can’t quite settle in my own body. It’s maybe not dramatic, but it’s been getting in the way when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do things like paperwork or talk through household plans. I just feel… uncomfortable and keyed up, I suppose. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been noticeably worse since I got married and our routines sort of merged… so, about a year and five months ago. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like it came out of nowhere, but that’s when it started feeling harder to ignore, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or urges to perform specific rituals?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in the sense of rituals, anyway. I might overthink things sometimes, but I don’t have, like, specific things I feel I have to repeat or do a certain way to make something bad not happen. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any involuntary movements or tics?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I fidget sometimes, but not like movements I can’t control or tics, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” exactly. I mean, I do worry about things sometimes, like anyone, but I don’t know that it feels like anxiety is the main thing. Maybe I’m not explaining that well… does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical sensations like a racing heart, sweating, or trembling when you feel restless?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. When I’m restless it’s more like… I just feel unsettled or like I need to move, but I haven’t noticed my heart racing or sweating or trembling. I mean, not in a way that stands out to me. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I avoid things exactly… but I do put off things where I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork, or talk through household plans. That’s kind of what brought me in, honestly — I feel like I can’t settle in my own body when I’m trying to do those things. I can still do them sometimes, but it feels really uncomfortable, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Not like the same scary thought over and over, I don’t think. It’s more that my attention gets pulled away really easily. Like if I’m at the kitchen table trying to get something done, my phone buzzes, or a car goes by outside, or I suddenly think about what we’re having for dinner, and then I’m completely off track before I even realize I stopped. I know that probably sounds normal, but it happens enough that it gets frustrating. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical complaints like pain or discomfort that you haven't mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nothing that feels like a separate physical problem I should be reporting. I get uncomfortable when I’m trying to sit still, but not pain exactly. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you feel compelled to think about over and over, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I can get stuck on things a little if I’m worried I forgot something, but not like I feel forced to think the same thought over and over. It’s more… my mind jumps around, but I don’t have a thought I’m trying to push away that keeps coming back. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or events that seem to trigger your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Mostly when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do the boring-but-important stuff, like paperwork or going over household plans with someone. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s when I really notice this feeling like I can’t quite settle in my own body. Like I should be able to just sit there and handle it, but I get restless really quickly. Is that what you mean by triggers?\n\nDoctor: Has your restlessness affected your relationship with your spouse or other family members since you got married?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t want to blame everything on that, but it has caused some strain. At home, I think people don’t always feel like they can count on me to follow through, even when I really do mean to. And that’s hard to admit.\n\nIt’s happened in volunteer things too, where I’ve had to step back from responsibilities because I kept letting things fall through. Not because I didn’t care—I was trying—but it still affected other people. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or criticize yourself when you can't settle down or complete tasks?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little, yes. I don’t know if I’d call it harsh criticism exactly, but I do get disappointed in myself when I can’t just sit down and finish something like I’m supposed to. I’ll think, “Why is this so hard?” or feel like I’m letting people down.\n\nI try not to dwell on it too much, but yes, there’s definitely some guilt there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you're in situations where you have to sit quietly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think fearful, no. Anxious… maybe a little sometimes, but I’m not sure that’s the main thing. It’s more like I get uncomfortable or impatient, like I’m trying to make myself stay put and my mind is saying, “Can we be done now?”\n\nI don’t want to overstate it. I’m not usually scared in those situations. It’s more that sitting quietly feels harder than it probably should, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself making excuses to get out of situations where you have to sit quietly?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… though I usually tell myself it’s for a practical reason, not just because I don’t want to sit there.\n\nIt’s more that when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do things like paperwork or go over household plans, I feel like I can’t really settle in my own body. I get this urge to get up or shift to something else. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but that’s honestly part of why I’m here. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel irritable when you're restless?\n\nPatient: Irritable… maybe somewhat, yes. I don’t think I snap at people constantly or anything, but when I’m restless and I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork or household plans, I can feel myself getting tense and short inside.\n\nIt’s more like I can’t settle in my own body, and then I get frustrated by that. I don’t know if that counts as irritability exactly, but it does make me feel more on edge.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid social gatherings or meetings because of your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Not really social gatherings, I don’t think… or at least I wouldn’t say I avoid them just because of restlessness.\n\nMeetings or things where I’m expected to sit quietly for a while, though, that’s harder. I may not avoid them outright, but I definitely dread them or try to keep them short. What brings me in is more that feeling like I can’t settle in my own body anymore, especially when I’m supposed to sit and deal with paperwork or shared household plans. I don’t know if that counts as avoidance exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical symptoms like headaches or stomachaches when you're feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Um… not that I’ve really noticed. I wouldn’t say I get headaches or stomachaches from it, at least not in a clear way.\n\nIt’s more just feeling unsettled or tense, but I don’t think I have those kinds of physical symptoms. Is that useful to know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness is more related to how you're feeling emotionally or to physical sensations in your body?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more… in my body, probably. I mean, emotions get mixed in because I get frustrated with myself, but the main thing is this feeling like I just can’t settle.\n\nEspecially if I’m supposed to sit quietly and do paperwork or talk through household plans, I feel like I’m kind of crawling out of my skin. Not dramatically, but enough that it’s hard to stay with it. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Maybe I’m not explaining that well… does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical sensations like a racing heart, sweating, or trembling when you feel restless?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. When I’m restless it’s more like… I just feel unsettled or like I need to move, but I haven’t noticed my heart racing or sweating or trembling. I mean, not in a way that stands out to me. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of this restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I avoid things exactly… but I do put off things where I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork, or talk through household plans. That’s kind of what brought me in, honestly — I feel like I can’t settle in my own body when I’m trying to do those things. I can still do them sometimes, but it feels really uncomfortable, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Not like the same scary thought over and over, I don’t think. It’s more that my attention gets pulled away really easily. Like if I’m at the kitchen table trying to get something done, my phone buzzes, or a car goes by outside, or I suddenly think about what we’re having for dinner, and then I’m completely off track before I even realize I stopped. I know that probably sounds normal, but it happens enough that it gets frustrating. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical complaints like pain or discomfort that you haven't mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nothing that feels like a separate physical problem I should be reporting. I get uncomfortable when I’m trying to sit still, but not pain exactly. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you feel compelled to think about over and over, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I can get stuck on things a little if I’m worried I forgot something, but not like I feel forced to think the same thought over and over. It’s more… my mind jumps around, but I don’t have a thought I’m trying to push away that keeps coming back. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or events that seem to trigger your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Mostly when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do the boring-but-important stuff, like paperwork or going over household plans with someone. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s when I really notice this feeling like I can’t quite settle in my own body. Like I should be able to just sit there and handle it, but I get restless really quickly. Is that what you mean by triggers?\n\nDoctor: Has your restlessness affected your relationship with your spouse or other family members since you got married?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t want to blame everything on that, but it has caused some strain. At home, I think people don’t always feel like they can count on me to follow through, even when I really do mean to. And that’s hard to admit.\n\nIt’s happened in volunteer things too, where I’ve had to step back from responsibilities because I kept letting things fall through. Not because I didn’t care—I was trying—but it still affected other people. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or criticize yourself when you can't settle down or complete tasks?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little, yes. I don’t know if I’d call it harsh criticism exactly, but I do get disappointed in myself when I can’t just sit down and finish something like I’m supposed to. I’ll think, “Why is this so hard?” or feel like I’m letting people down.\n\nI try not to dwell on it too much, but yes, there’s definitely some guilt there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you're in situations where you have to sit quietly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think fearful, no. Anxious… maybe a little sometimes, but I’m not sure that’s the main thing. It’s more like I get uncomfortable or impatient, like I’m trying to make myself stay put and my mind is saying, “Can we be done now?”\n\nI don’t want to overstate it. I’m not usually scared in those situations. It’s more that sitting quietly feels harder than it probably should, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself making excuses to get out of situations where you have to sit quietly?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… though I usually tell myself it’s for a practical reason, not just because I don’t want to sit there.\n\nIt’s more that when I’m supposed to sit quietly and do things like paperwork or go over household plans, I feel like I can’t really settle in my own body. I get this urge to get up or shift to something else. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but that’s honestly part of why I’m here. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel irritable when you're restless?\n\nPatient: Irritable… maybe somewhat, yes. I don’t think I snap at people constantly or anything, but when I’m restless and I’m supposed to just sit quietly and deal with paperwork or household plans, I can feel myself getting tense and short inside.\n\nIt’s more like I can’t settle in my own body, and then I get frustrated by that. I don’t know if that counts as irritability exactly, but it does make me feel more on edge.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid social gatherings or meetings because of your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Not really social gatherings, I don’t think… or at least I wouldn’t say I avoid them just because of restlessness.\n\nMeetings or things where I’m expected to sit quietly for a while, though, that’s harder. I may not avoid them outright, but I definitely dread them or try to keep them short. What brings me in is more that feeling like I can’t settle in my own body anymore, especially when I’m supposed to sit and deal with paperwork or shared household plans. I don’t know if that counts as avoidance exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical symptoms like headaches or stomachaches when you're feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Um… not that I’ve really noticed. I wouldn’t say I get headaches or stomachaches from it, at least not in a clear way.\n\nIt’s more just feeling unsettled or tense, but I don’t think I have those kinds of physical symptoms. Is that useful to know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness is more related to how you're feeling emotionally or to physical sensations in your body?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more… in my body, probably. I mean, emotions get mixed in because I get frustrated with myself, but the main thing is this feeling like I just can’t settle.\n\nEspecially if I’m supposed to sit quietly and do paperwork or talk through household plans, I feel like I’m kind of crawling out of my skin. Not dramatically, but enough that it’s hard to stay with it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has your restlessness been consistently affecting your daily activities and relationships over the past year?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I really do try, but over the past year it’s been affecting things pretty consistently.\n\nAt home, and even in some volunteer stuff I’m involved with, people can’t always count on me the way I’d like them to. I’ll mean to follow through, and then things kind of fall through anyway. I’ve had to step back from some responsibilities because of that, which is embarrassing to admit. But yes, I guess it has been affecting daily life and relationships."
